## Idocrase

**Id:** `idocrase`

**Alt names:** Vesuvianite, Giok Lumut, Bio-Solar, Solar, Lumut Aceh, Sungai Dareh, Totol Sayur, Es Jade

**Mineral family:** Vesuvianite (sorosilicate)

**Chemical formula:** Ca₁₀Mg₂Al₄(SiO₄)₅(Si₂O₇)₂(OH)₄

**Crystal system:** Tetragonal

**Mohs hardness:** 6.5

**Luster:** Vitreous to resinous

**Transparency:** Transparent to translucent

**Color range:** Olive to emerald green, yellow, brown and brown-black; Aceh "Bio-Solar" is brown with green, "Solar" is brown without green. Rarely white, red, violet or blue-green.

**Origins:** Indonesia (Beutong / Gunung Singgah Mata, Nagan Raya, Aceh), Indonesia (Gayo Lues, Aceh), Indonesia (Sungai Dareh, West Sumatra), Italy (Mount Vesuvius, type locality), Canada, Pakistan, Kenya, United States

**Rarity:** Uncommon

**Ethical sourcing:** Much Aceh idocrase is dug in the Beutong area, part of which is protected forest (hutan lindung). Mining there without a permit is illegal and damages the forest. During the batu akik boom, seekers reported earning over Rp20 million per month, which drove heavy digging. Ask the seller where the stone was mined.

**Simulant warnings:** Idocrase can look like jade, peridot, tourmaline, zircon or garnet. It is itself often sold as "giok" (jade) in Aceh, but it is not jade (not jadeite or nephrite). A gem lab separates it by refractive index (about 1.70–1.72) and specific gravity (about 3.3–3.4), which are higher than nephrite or serpentine. At home, a hydrostatic weighing (SG test) can separate it from glass and serpentine. Most idocrase is not treated, but no lab study confirms this for Aceh material.

**Short intention:** Release old fear and grow toward courage

**Intention:** Courage & Release

**Chakras:** Heart, Solar Plexus

**Element:** Earth

**Planet:** Jupiter

**Day of week:** Thursday

**Zodiac:** Sagittarius, Capricorn

**Numerology:** 4

**Polarity:** Yang

**Solfeggio frequency:** 639 Hz

**Energy type:** Grounding

**Emotional:**
- Helps release feelings of being trapped or held back
- Encourages courage to change a stuck situation
- Supports letting go of old anger and resentment
- Brings a steady, hopeful mood

**Mental:**
- Supports clear decisions after a long period of doubt
- Helps break repeating negative thought patterns
- Encourages practical planning for change
- Supports focus on long-term goals

**Spiritual:**
- Links the Heart with personal will (Solar Plexus)
- Associated in crystal practice with connecting to one's higher purpose
- Supports spiritual growth that is grounded in daily life
- A forest-green stone carrying the energy of the Sumatran mountains

**Physical:**
- Traditionally associated with supporting vitality and recovery
- Associated in crystal practice with the digestive system
- Linked in folk practice with strength and endurance
- These are folk and metaphysical beliefs, not medical claims

**Vedic tradition:** No classical Jyotish basis. Idocrase is not a navaratna or a recognized upratna.

**Tcm use:** No traditional Chinese materia medica use found.

**Five element theory:** Corresponds to Wood through its green colour and growing, forest-like energy, with Earth for its grounding nature.

**Feng shui bagua:** Family & Health (Zhen), Wealth & Prosperity (Xun)

**Feng shui placement:** East or southeast areas of the home for growth and health. Near plants or in a green corner. On a desk when planning a big change.

**Ancient lore:** The mineral was first described from Mount Vesuvius, Italy, which gave it the name vesuvianite. The name idocrase comes from Greek words meaning "mixed form", because its crystals look like those of other minerals. In Indonesia, idocrase became famous during the batu akik boom of 2014–2015, when Aceh "giok" was traded nationwide.

**Indigenous traditions:** An ethnographic study of Beutong Ateuh, Nagan Raya, records ritual practices by local seekers when hunting giok in the Singgah Mata mountains. A Bio-Solar stone from Nagan Raya won first place in a national gemstone contest. Local names describe the look of the stone: "lumut" (moss), "solar" (diesel-fuel colour), "totol sayur" (vegetable spots).

**Meditation guidance:** Hold idocrase between both hands at the solar plexus. Name one situation where you feel stuck. With each out-breath, picture green light loosening the hold of that situation. With each in-breath, draw in courage. End by placing the stone over the heart and choosing one small, real step to take.

**Body placement:** Solar plexus for courage; heart for release; worn as a ring or pendant.

**Space placement:** East side of the home. Office or study during times of change. Near plants.

**Elixir safety:** Indirect

**Elixir notes:** Vesuvianite is almost insoluble in acids, but Aceh Bio-Solar contains about 1.3% nickel oxide (XRF study). Use the indirect method only.

**Jewelry recommendations:** Good for rings, pendants and cabochons (hardness 6.5). It is brittle with poor cleavage, so protect from hard knocks.

**Safe cleansing:**
- Running water
- Mild soap and water
- Moonlight
- Sound bath
- Smudging
- Earth burial (brief)

**Unsafe cleansing:**
- Hard knocks or tumbling (brittle)
- Ultrasonic cleaners (risk to fractured stones)

**Charging methods:**
- Morning sunlight (short)
- Earth contact
- Placing among green plants
- Sound frequency 639 Hz

**Storage guidance:** Store in a soft pouch away from harder stones such as quartz and sapphire. Protect from drops.

**Cautions:** Brittle — avoid drops and knocks. Contains a small amount of nickel in some Aceh material; people with nickel allergy should avoid long skin contact.

**Toxicity:** Low toxicity. Some Aceh material contains about 1.3% nickel oxide; avoid ingestion and dust from cutting.

**Fragility:** Moderate — hardness 6.5 but brittle with poor cleavage.

**Synergies:**
- Jade (`jade`): Idocrase is sold as "giok" in Aceh. Pairing it with true jade joins jade's calm, long-lasting harmony with idocrase's courage to change.
- Peridot (`peridot`): Both are green stones of the Heart and Solar Plexus. Peridot clears old emotional patterns; idocrase gives the courage to act after the release.
- Klawing Jasper (`klawing-jasper`): Two Indonesian earth stones. Klawing jasper grounds and protects; idocrase moves the heart toward change. Together they support steady, safe transformation.

**Conflicts:**
- Lepidolite (`lepidolite`): Lepidolite slows things down and soothes; idocrase pushes for release and change. Used together, the message can feel mixed.

**Grid role:** Courage stone in change and release grids; Heart-Solar Plexus bridge in growth layouts

## Aceh Black Jade

**Id:** `aceh-black-jade`

**Alt names:** Giok Hitam Aceh, Giok Hitam, Serpentine Jade, Black Serpentine, Antigorite, Giok Serpentin

**Mineral family:** Serpentine (antigorite) — not true jade

**Chemical formula:** (Mg,Fe²⁺)₃Si₂O₅(OH)₄ (antigorite)

**Crystal system:** Monoclinic (antigorite)

**Mohs hardness:** 2.5–5.5

**Luster:** Resinous, greasy, waxy to silky

**Transparency:** Translucent to opaque

**Color range:** Black, blackish green, dark green; Aceh serpentine also occurs in yellowish green and brown

**Origins:** Indonesia (Aceh Tengah / Takengon, Aceh), Indonesia (Nagan Raya and Beutong, Aceh)

**Rarity:** Common

**Ethical sourcing:** Aceh black jade is dug in the same Beutong area as idocrase, part of which is protected forest. Mining without a permit is illegal there. Serpentine rock can contain chrysotile (asbestos) fibres, so cutting and polishing workers face a dust risk without water and masks. No other labor or environmental data was found.

**Simulant warnings:** Aceh black jade is itself a trade name: the stone is serpentine, not jadeite or nephrite. The serpentine ID comes from one XRF study only; the high iron content (35–39% Fe₂O₃) suggests iron-oxide inclusions such as magnetite. Black glass, black-dyed stones and other dark stones are sold under the same name. A gem lab separates serpentine by specific gravity (about 2.6) and refractive index (about 1.56), which are lower than nephrite or jadeite. Raman spectroscopy or XRD confirms the mineral. Some sellers say the stone is magnetic; this is not verified but would point to magnetite inclusions.

**Indigenous traditions:** Local sellers and blogs say the stone brings health benefits and psychological calm; this is a modern local belief. Aceh traders sell it under many names, including "Badar Lumut Aceh", "Giok Sumatera", "Giok China" and "Giok Aswad".

**Elixir safety:** Avoid

**Elixir notes:** Do not use for elixirs. The stone contains 2–2.6% nickel oxide (XRF study), and Aceh serpentine rock can contain chrysotile (asbestos) fibres.

**Jewelry recommendations:** Soft (hardness about 2.5–5.5) — best as pendants or carved pieces, not rings for daily wear. Contains nickel; people with nickel allergy should avoid long skin contact.

**Safe cleansing:**
- Wipe with a damp cloth, then dry
- Moonlight
- Sound bath
- Smudging
- Visualization

**Unsafe cleansing:**
- Dry sanding or cutting (dust and possible asbestos fibres)
- Heat
- Abrasive cloths (scratches easily)
- Salt (abrasive)
- Ultrasonic cleaners

**Charging methods:**
- Earth contact
- Moonlight
- Sound frequency 396 Hz
- Black tourmaline nearby

**Storage guidance:** Store separately in a soft pouch; it scratches easily. Keep away from heat.

**Cautions:** Never cut, sand or polish it dry — serpentine dust can contain chrysotile (asbestos) fibres. Contains nickel (2–2.6% NiO): avoid long skin contact if you have a nickel allergy. Not true jade.

**Toxicity:** Contains nickel; possible chrysotile (asbestos) fibres in Aceh serpentine rock. Safe to handle when polished and intact; never create dust.

**Fragility:** Soft — hardness 2.5–5.5 with perfect cleavage. Scratches easily.

**Synergies:**
- Black Tourmaline (`black-tourmaline`): Both are black protective stones of the Root. Black tourmaline deflects; Aceh black jade settles and calms. Together they form a strong protection pair.
- Jade (`jade`): Pairs the "jade" name with the real jade it is sold as. True jade brings harmony; serpentine brings protection and calm.
- Smoky Quartz (`smoky-quartz`): Both ground and protect. Smoky quartz transmutes heavy energy; Aceh black jade holds a calm, safe space.

**Conflicts:**
- Moldavite (`moldavite`): Moldavite brings fast, intense change; Aceh black jade brings stillness and protection. Together the effect can feel unsettled.

**Grid role:** Corner protection stone in shielding grids; Root anchor in calming layouts

## Klawing Jasper

**Id:** `klawing-jasper`

**Alt names:** Batu Klawing, Nogo Sui, Pancawarna Purbalingga, Pancawati, Telur Kodok

**Mineral family:** Jasper (microcrystalline quartz)

**Chemical formula:** SiO₂ + impurities (iron oxides give the red colour)

**Crystal system:** Trigonal (microcrystalline quartz)

**Mohs hardness:** 6.5

**Luster:** Dull to vitreous when polished

**Transparency:** Opaque; translucent pieces grade into chalcedony

**Color range:** Green, red, yellow, brown and multicolour; Pancawarna is green-blue with layers and red spots

**Origins:** Indonesia (Klawing River, Bobotsari, Purbalingga, Central Java)

**Rarity:** Common

**Ethical sourcing:** Klawing jasper occurs as veins in basalt lava near Limbasari village and as river stones in the Klawing River terraces. Sand and gravel mining in the river destroys gem stones, according to local reports. Green-jasper artefacts also occur along the Klawing River: do not collect or export artefacts, because cultural-heritage objects are on Indonesia's prohibited-export list.

**Simulant warnings:** "Klawing" is the river name; Nogo Sui, Pancawarna and Pancawati are pattern or trade names. No Klawing-specific fakes have been reported. As with other chalcedony and jasper, dyed material exists: wipe with an acetone swab and look for colour, and check cracks under a loupe for concentrated dye. Red-spotted green Klawing material resembles bloodstone (heliotrope) and is sometimes sold under that name.

**Short intention:** Stand firm on home ground

**Intention:** Grounding & Endurance

**Chakras:** Root, Heart

**Element:** Earth

**Planet:** Mars

**Day of week:** Tuesday

**Zodiac:** Aries, Virgo

**Numerology:** 6

**Polarity:** Yang

**Solfeggio frequency:** 396 Hz

**Energy type:** Grounding

**Emotional:**
- Brings stability and a sense of belonging
- Supports patience and endurance in hard times
- Calms emotional ups and downs
- Encourages loyalty and reliability

**Mental:**
- Supports practical thinking and steady work
- Helps finish long tasks
- Encourages organization and order
- Supports clear, realistic goals

**Spiritual:**
- Connects to the land and to local, ancestral roots
- Associated in crystal practice with jasper as the "supreme nurturer"
- Protects while staying open-hearted (green types)
- A river stone of Central Java carrying the energy of moving water and earth

**Physical:**
- Traditionally associated with stamina and endurance
- Associated in crystal practice with jasper and general vitality
- Linked in folk practice with recovery after illness
- These are folk and metaphysical beliefs, not medical claims

**Vedic tradition:** No classical Jyotish basis for jasper.

**Tcm use:** No traditional Chinese materia medica use found.

**Five element theory:** Corresponds to Earth (stability) with Wood in green types and Fire in red types.

**Feng shui bagua:** Knowledge & Wisdom (Gen), Family & Health (Zhen)

**Feng shui placement:** Northeast area of the home for steady self-improvement. In the family room for stability. Large river pieces work well in gardens.

**Ancient lore:** Green-jasper artefacts have been found along the Klawing River, showing that people used this stone long ago; the published report does not give their culture or date. Bloodstone, the red-spotted green type of jasper/chalcedony, is the March birthstone on the 1912 American jewelers' list.

**Indigenous traditions:** Klawing jasper is a regional identity stone of Purbalingga, Central Java. Local collectors hunt stones in the Klawing River and name them by pattern: "Nogo Sui" (dragon pattern), "Pancawarna" (five colours) and "Telur Kodok" (frog egg). No documented folk rituals specific to Klawing jasper were found.

**Meditation guidance:** Hold Klawing jasper while sitting with your feet flat on the ground. Picture the river: water moving, stones staying in place. With each breath, let your body be the stone — steady while life moves around you. Stay until you feel heavy and calm.

**Body placement:** Root for grounding; heart for green types; carried in a pocket for daily stability.

**Space placement:** Family room or entrance. Garden or plant pots. A study desk for steady work.

**Elixir safety:** Generally Safe

**Elixir notes:** Jasper is silica with iron oxides and has low solubility. Clean, polished pieces can be used directly; the indirect method is a sensible precaution for rough river stones.

**Jewelry recommendations:** Durable (hardness about 6.5–7) — good for rings, bracelets, pendants and cabochons for daily wear.

**Safe cleansing:**
- Running water
- Mild soap and water
- Earth burial
- Moonlight
- Sunlight
- Smudging
- Sound bath

**Unsafe cleansing:**
- Acid cleaners (can affect iron-oxide colours)

**Charging methods:**
- Earth contact
- Running river water
- Sunlight
- Sound frequency 396 Hz

**Storage guidance:** Durable; store with other stones of similar hardness. Keep polished pieces in a pouch to protect the shine.

**Cautions:** Avoid breathing silica dust when cutting or polishing. Do not collect or export archaeological artefacts from the Klawing River.

**Toxicity:** Non-toxic; safe to handle. Silica dust from cutting is a lung hazard.

**Fragility:** Low — tough and durable.

**Synergies:**
- Red Jasper (`red-jasper`): Both are jaspers of the Root. Red jasper energizes and Klawing jasper steadies; together they give lasting, calm stamina.
- Idocrase (`idocrase`): Two Indonesian earth stones. Klawing jasper grounds; idocrase gives the courage to change. Together they support safe, steady growth.
- Green Aventurine (`green-aventurine`): Green Klawing jasper and green aventurine both work at the Heart. Aventurine invites opportunity; jasper keeps you steady enough to use it.

**Conflicts:**
- Moldavite (`moldavite`): Moldavite brings sudden, high-intensity change; Klawing jasper brings slow, steady stability. Together they can feel like they pull in opposite directions.

**Grid role:** Foundation and corner stone in grounding grids; anchor stone in Indonesian-stone layouts

## Akar Bahar (Black Coral)

**Id:** `akar-bahar`

**Alt names:** Akar Bahar, Koral Hitam, Black Coral, Antipathes, King's Coral

**Mineral family:** Organic — black coral (Antipatharia)

**Chemical formula:** Organic (protein + chitin)

**Crystal system:** None (organic)

**Mohs hardness:** 3

**Luster:** Waxy to resinous when polished

**Transparency:** Opaque

**Color range:** Black to dark brown; sometimes bleached to gold ("golden coral")

**Origins:** Oceans worldwide (2 to 8,600 m deep), Indonesia (Alor, East Nusa Tenggara), Indonesia (Banggai, Central Sulawesi)

**Rarity:** Rare

**Ethical sourcing:** PROTECTED SPECIES — DO NOT BUY OR SELL. All black corals (Antipatharia) have been listed in CITES Appendix II since 1981, so international trade needs CITES permits. In Indonesia, PP 7/1999 (annex row 222) protects "Antipathes spp. — Akar bahar, Koral hitam", and Law 5/1990 (amended by Law 32/2024) bans taking, keeping and trading protected species; this covers craftsmen and traders as well as collectors. Black corals grow extremely slowly — some deep-sea colonies are estimated to be over 2,000 years old — and over-collection for jewellery led to the CITES listing. Illegal collection still happens in Alor. This entry exists for identification and legal awareness only.

**Simulant warnings:** Black coral is often coated with thick resin or black lacquer (SSEF, GIA). Black-coated horn coral, plastic, glass and dyed materials are sold as akar bahar. In Indonesia, "akar bahar" also names gorgonian sea fans (Rumphella, Hicksonella), so many "akar bahar" bracelets are not black coral at all. Real black coral shows concentric "tree-ring" growth layers on cut ends and small spine dimples on the surface. A hot-needle test gives a burnt-protein smell on horn coral and melts plastic — but because the material is protected, the right action is not to buy it.

**Short intention:** Respect the sea — protect, do not possess

**Intention:** Protection & Respect for Nature

**Chakras:** Root

**Element:** Water

**Planet:** Saturn

**Day of week:** Saturday

**Zodiac:** Scorpio, Capricorn

**Numerology:** 8

**Polarity:** Yin

**Solfeggio frequency:** 396 Hz

**Energy type:** Protective

**Emotional:**
- In regional belief, gives a feeling of safety and protection
- Symbolizes endurance through very slow growth
- Invites respect and care for living things
- Reminds the wearer that real protection includes protecting nature

**Mental:**
- Encourages awareness of the law and of conservation
- Supports patient, long-term thinking
- Teaches the difference between a mineral and a living animal
- Encourages checking what you buy before you buy it

**Spiritual:**
- In Alor, believed to ward off black magic (menangkal ilmu gaib)
- Very old pieces are believed to protect the body
- A living sea animal, not a mineral — its "energy" is the life of the reef
- Modern practice: honour it by protecting it in the sea, not by wearing it

**Physical:**
- Used in regional folk practice as a remedy for rheumatism
- Some gorgonian "akar bahar" contains radium (IPB study) — another reason not to wear it
- No medical evidence supports any health use
- These are folk beliefs, not medical claims

**Vedic tradition:** No classical basis for black coral. The navaratna coral (praval, a Mars stone) is red coral, not black coral.

**Tcm use:** No traditional Chinese materia medica use found for black coral.

**Five element theory:** Corresponds to Water — a living animal of the deep sea.

**Feng shui bagua:** Not recommended — do not use protected species in feng shui.

**Feng shui placement:** Not recommended. Do not buy or display protected coral. Use black tourmaline or obsidian for protection instead.

**Ancient lore:** Black coral has a long history as a protective amulet in many sea cultures. In Hawaii, black coral (ʻēkaha kū moana) is the state gem. No scholarly source for ancient Indonesian lore was found.

**Indigenous traditions:** In Alor, East Nusa Tenggara, akar bahar is believed to ward off black magic, and pieces "hundreds of years old" are believed to protect the body (Mahkamah journal study). It is also used as a folk remedy for rheumatism. The same study found that illegal collection continues because of low income, weak supervision and low legal awareness.

**Meditation guidance:** Do not use akar bahar for meditation. For a sea-protection meditation, hold a legal stone such as black tourmaline or larimar, picture the deep ocean and its slow-growing coral forests, and set an intention to protect them.

**Body placement:** Not recommended. Wearing akar bahar bracelets supports illegal trade of a protected species.

**Space placement:** Not recommended. Do not display protected coral.

**Elixir safety:** Avoid

**Elixir notes:** Never use for elixirs. It is organic and can degrade; it may be coated with resin; some gorgonian akar bahar contains radium (IPB study). It is also illegal to possess as a protected species in Indonesia.

**Jewelry recommendations:** Do not buy or wear. Akar bahar bracelets are the main illegal trade item. If you already own a piece, do not sell or give it away; ask the local BKSDA (Natural Resources Conservation Agency) for advice.

**Safe cleansing:**
- Not applicable — do not use protected coral

**Unsafe cleansing:**
- Heat (organic; burns)
- Solvents such as acetone (remove coatings and dyes)
- Long soaking

**Charging methods:**
- Not applicable — do not use protected coral

**Storage guidance:** If you already own a piece, keep it dry and away from heat. Do not sell, trade or export it.

**Cautions:** PROTECTED SPECIES: CITES Appendix II and Indonesian PP 7/1999. Buying, selling, keeping or exporting it can break Indonesian law. Many items labelled "akar bahar" are gorgonians, which are also covered by KKP protection plans. The status of black coral in the current annex (Permen LHK P.106/2018) was not verified.

**Toxicity:** Radium reported in gorgonian akar bahar (IPB study). Resin coatings unknown. Do not ingest.

**Fragility:** Organic; damaged by heat, solvents and long soaking.

**Synergies:**
- Black Tourmaline (`black-tourmaline`): Use black tourmaline instead of akar bahar. It gives the same protective intention without harming a protected species.
- Obsidian (`obsidian`): A legal, mineral alternative for protection against negative energy, the main use of akar bahar in folk belief.
- Larimar (`larimar`): For a sea connection, larimar carries ocean energy without taking anything living from the reef.

**Conflicts:** none

**Grid role:** Not for use in grids — reference entry for identification and legal awareness only

## Opal

**Id:** `opal`

**Alt names:** Precious Opal, Fire Opal, Black Opal, Jelly Opal, Welo Opal, Kalimaya, Opal Banten

**Mineral family:** Mineraloid (hydrated silica)

**Chemical formula:** SiO₂·nH₂O

**Crystal system:** Amorphous (opal-A) to poorly ordered (opal-CT)

**Mohs hardness:** 5.5

**Luster:** Vitreous to waxy

**Transparency:** Transparent to opaque

**Color range:** Body colour from colourless and white to black. Precious opal shows play of colour: flashes of spectral colours that move as the stone turns.

**Origins:** Australia (Lightning Ridge, Coober Pedy), Ethiopia (Welo, since 2008), Indonesia (Lebak, Banten — see Kalimaya Opal), Mexico

**Rarity:** Uncommon

**Ethical sourcing:** Much opal comes from small-scale and hand mining. In Banten, Indonesia, local people dig shafts more than ten metres deep by hand. No published labour or safety data was found for the main opal fields, so ask sellers about the source. Opal is a mineral, so CITES and Indonesian protected-species rules do not apply.

**Simulant warnings:** Opal has many imitations. (1) Synthetic (Gilson) opal, sold since 1974: under magnification it shows a "snake skin" or "lizard skin" pattern of colour patches. (2) Polymer-impregnated synthetic opal and opal-like plastic ("Oparex"), which is mostly resin. (3) Doublets (a thin opal slice glued to a dark backing) and triplets (the same with a clear dome on top): look at the side of the stone for a flat, straight glue line; a triplet top looks glassy. (4) Natural opal impregnated with black plastic, and dyed Welo opal, which is porous and takes dye easily. Labs (GIA, SSEF) use microscopy and infrared spectroscopy. "Kalimaya" is a local trade name for precious opal, not a separate mineral.

**Short intention:** Let every colour of the self be seen

**Intention:** Creativity & Self-Expression

**Chakras:** Sacral, Crown

**Element:** Water

**Planet:** Venus

**Day of week:** Friday

**Zodiac:** Libra, Cancer, Scorpio

**Numerology:** 8

**Polarity:** Yin

**Solfeggio frequency:** 417 Hz

**Energy type:** Amplifying

**Emotional:**
- Encourages emotional honesty and free expression
- Supports confidence in one's own uniqueness
- Lifts the mood when life feels flat
- Helps feelings surface in a gentle, colourful way

**Mental:**
- Stimulates imagination and original ideas
- Supports artists, writers and designers
- Helps break creative blocks
- Encourages seeing many possibilities at once

**Spiritual:**
- Amplifies the intention or emotion it is used with, in crystal tradition
- Links creative power (Sacral) with inspiration (Crown)
- Supports dream work and visionary states
- Symbolizes the many colours of light held in one stone

**Physical:**
- Traditionally associated with vitality and creative energy
- Associated in crystal practice with restful sleep and dream recall
- Folk tradition links opal with eye health
- These are folk and metaphysical beliefs, not medical claims

**Vedic tradition:** In modern Indian gem practice, retailers list opal as an upratna (substitute) for diamond, the gem of Venus (Shukra). No classical text basis was found.

**Tcm use:** No traditional Chinese materia medica use found.

**Five element theory:** Corresponds to Water (its hydrated nature) carrying Fire (its play of colour).

**Feng shui bagua:** Children & Creativity (Dui), Fame & Reputation (Li)

**Feng shui placement:** West area of the home for creativity. In a studio or creative workspace. Keep away from heaters, lamps and windows with strong sun.

**Ancient lore:** Opal is the October birthstone on the modern American list set by the jewellers' association in Kansas City in 1912; tourmaline was added for October in 1952. Synthetic opal entered the market in 1974, when Pierre Gilson began selling it.

**Indigenous traditions:** In Indonesia, Banten miners use "kalimaya" for opal with play of colour and "opal" only for white opal without it. The name comes from Kali Maja, the place where it was first found. No documented Indonesian folk beliefs about opal were found.

**Meditation guidance:** Sit in soft light and turn the opal slowly in your fingers. Watch each flash of colour appear and fade. Let each colour stand for one part of yourself you want to express. Then close your eyes and picture those colours glowing below the navel and rising to the crown. Write down any ideas that come.

**Body placement:** Below the navel for creativity; at the crown for inspiration; worn as a pendant or earrings.

**Space placement:** Studio, workshop or creative desk. Away from direct sun, heaters and very dry air.

**Elixir safety:** Use with Caution

**Elixir notes:** Water does not dissolve opal, but hydrophane opal (such as Welo) takes up liquid and changes appearance, and the glue in doublets and triplets fails in water. Use the indirect method only (stone in a sealed glass container).

**Jewelry recommendations:** Best in pendants, earrings and protected bezel settings. Hardness about 5.5–6.5, so avoid daily rings that take knocks. Remove before washing, cooking or applying lotion. Never soak doublets or triplets.

**Safe cleansing:**
- Dry, soft cloth
- Moonlight
- Sound bath
- Visualization
- Smudging

**Unsafe cleansing:**
- Soaking in water, oils or lotions (hydrophane opal takes them up)
- Soaking doublets or triplets (glue fails)
- Heat
- Fast drying after wetting (can cause crazing)
- Ultrasonic and steam cleaners

**Charging methods:**
- Moonlight
- Sound frequency 417 Hz
- Creative work done while wearing it
- Clear quartz nearby

**Storage guidance:** Store alone in a soft pouch, away from heat, strong light and harder stones. Avoid long storage in very dry places.

**Cautions:** Opal can crack ("craze") when it dries too fast or meets heat. Doublets, triplets and synthetic opal are common; ask the seller what you are buying.

**Toxicity:** Non-toxic (silica). Cutting dust is a respirable silica hazard.

**Fragility:** Moderately fragile — hardness 5.5–6.5; sensitive to knocks, heat and fast drying.

**Synergies:**
- Kalimaya Opal (`kalimaya`): Kalimaya is Indonesia's own precious opal from Banten. Pairing it with opal from other fields honours one stone family across many lands.
- Labradorite (`labradorite`): Both flash colour from within. Labradorite protects the aura while opal amplifies creative expression — a strong pair for artists who feel exposed.
- Moonstone (`moonstone`): Both are Water stones of dreams and feeling. Moonstone steadies; opal amplifies and inspires.

**Conflicts:**
- Shungite (`shungite`): Shungite absorbs and neutralizes energy; opal amplifies it. Used together, they can work against each other.

**Grid role:** Amplifier and centre stone in creativity grids

## Amber

**Id:** `amber`

**Alt names:** Succinite, Baltic Amber, Blue Amber, Indonesian Blue Amber, Sumatran Amber, Borneo Amber, Burmite, Succinum, Hu Po (琥珀)

**Mineral family:** Organic — fossil tree resin

**Chemical formula:** No fixed formula (polymerised resin; Baltic amber contains succinic acid)

**Crystal system:** None (organic, amorphous)

**Mohs hardness:** 2.5

**Luster:** Resinous

**Transparency:** Transparent to opaque

**Color range:** Yellow, honey and brown (classic). Also red, green and orange. Dominican and Indonesian "blue amber" glows blue under UV light and daylight. GIA found that the blue of Indonesian blue amber is UV-stimulated fluorescence confined to the surface; the body colour is red-brown or yellow.

**Origins:** Indonesia (Sumatra — Jambi, Bukit Asam, West Sumatra; also Borneo, Sulawesi, New Guinea), Baltic region, Dominican Republic, Myanmar (Kachin State)

**Rarity:** Common

**Ethical sourcing:** AVOID BURMESE AMBER. Burmese amber comes from Kachin State, Myanmar, where armed conflict has continued since 1962. A 2018 UN fact-finding mission reported forced labour and sexual violence by the military in mining areas, and the army took control of the amber mines in 2017. In 2020 the Society of Vertebrate Paleontology asked journals to stop publishing amber obtained after 2017. Indonesian amber is mostly a by-product of coal mining: villagers collect it from coal waste piles. Baltic and Dominican amber are the safer choices. Indonesian amber belongs to the glessite group and comes from dipterocarp trees (genus disputed: Dipterocarpus vs Shorea). Indonesia bans export of palaeontological collections with special scientific or cultural value (Permendag 22/2023, HS 97.05); ordinary gem amber is likely not covered, but a scientifically important fossil inclusion could be.

**Simulant warnings:** Copal (young resin) is the main imitation; GIA treats it as a separate material. Plastic, glass, pressed amber (small pieces fused under heat) and heat-treated "green amber" are also sold. Home tests do not prove much: in GIA's 2009 study, the saltwater float test, the hot-point test and UV light did not separate amber from copal well. Clues: copal glows whiter under short-wave UV; acetone makes copal sticky and softens plastic, while amber resists; pressed amber shows flow lines and long bubbles under magnification. Labs use infrared and NMR spectroscopy. For Indonesian amber, a mindat expert notes that material on sale is "some true amber but also much young polymerised resin", and that almost all amber and much copal fluoresce, so a blue glow under UV alone proves nothing. Copal with UV dyes or coatings is also sold as blue amber.

**Short intention:** Hold warmth, light and old wisdom

**Intention:** Warmth & Vitality

**Chakras:** Sacral, Solar Plexus

**Element:** Fire

**Planet:** Sun

**Day of week:** Sunday

**Zodiac:** Leo, Aquarius

**Numerology:** 3

**Polarity:** Yang

**Solfeggio frequency:** 528 Hz

**Energy type:** Activating

**Emotional:**
- Brings warmth, cheer and optimism
- Helps lift heavy or anxious moods
- Encourages self-confidence
- Supports a sunny, open disposition

**Mental:**
- Supports clear decisions and positive thinking
- Encourages patience and perspective
- Associated with memory and old wisdom
- Helps balance intellect with feeling

**Spiritual:**
- A gift of ancient trees, linking the wearer to deep time
- Carries the light and warmth of the sun
- Associated in crystal practice with clearing and protection
- Links creative energy (Sacral) with personal power (Solar Plexus)

**Physical:**
- In traditional Chinese medicine, Hu Po is used to "calm the Spirit" and stop palpitations
- Folk tradition links amber with soothing pain
- Associated in crystal practice with vitality
- These are traditional beliefs, not medical claims

**Vedic tradition:** No classical Jyotish basis for amber.

**Tcm use:** Named in the Chinese materia medica as Hu Po 琥珀 (Succinum), first recorded under this name in the Ming Yi Bie Lu. It is described as sweet and neutral, entering the Heart, Liver and Bladder channels. It is used to "calm the Spirit", stop palpitations and promote urination, taken as powder or pill, not boiled. This is a traditional belief system, not clinical medicine; a practitioner source warns it may increase the effect of sedatives.

**Five element theory:** Corresponds to Fire (its warmth and golden light) and Wood (its origin as tree resin).

**Feng shui bagua:** Fame & Reputation (Li), Wealth & Prosperity (Xun)

**Feng shui placement:** South area of the home for warmth and recognition. Living rooms where people gather. Keep away from heaters and direct sun.

**Ancient lore:** The Chinese name "hu po" comes from a legend that a tiger's soul enters the earth after death and turns into amber. Amber has been carved and traded since prehistoric times, and its ability to preserve insects and plants has made it valuable to science.

**Indigenous traditions:** No documented Indonesian folk beliefs about amber were found. In Indonesia, amber is mainly collected by villagers from coal waste piles, and "Borneo amber" is used as a trade name.

**Meditation guidance:** Hold amber in the palm of your hand until it warms. Picture sunlight caught inside it millions of years ago. Breathe that warm, golden light into the belly and the solar plexus. Let it melt tension and fear. End by giving thanks to the ancient forest the amber came from.

**Body placement:** Solar plexus for confidence; below the navel for creativity; worn as beads or a pendant.

**Space placement:** Living room or family area. South side of the home. Away from heat sources and solvents.

**Elixir safety:** Generally Safe

**Elixir notes:** Polished amber is suitable for brief contact. It is soft and reacts to solvents, so do not use alcohol-based elixirs. The indirect method is the safer choice.

**Jewelry recommendations:** Light and warm to wear — good for beads, pendants and earrings. Very soft (about 2–2.5), so it scratches easily; store separately. Put on perfume and hairspray before wearing amber.

**Safe cleansing:**
- Soft dry cloth
- Moonlight
- Sound bath
- Visualization
- Smudging (at a distance)

**Unsafe cleansing:**
- Heat or open flame
- Solvents (acetone, alcohol, perfume)
- Abrasives and salt
- Ultrasonic cleaners
- Long sun exposure

**Charging methods:**
- Gentle morning sunlight (short)
- Holding it until it warms
- Sound frequency 528 Hz
- Citrine nearby

**Storage guidance:** Store separately in a soft pouch, away from harder stones, heat and chemicals.

**Cautions:** Very soft and heat-sensitive. Avoid Burmese amber because of documented conflict and forced-labour links. Copal and plastic are often sold as amber.

**Toxicity:** Non-toxic in polished form. Do not ingest amber as a supplement without professional advice.

**Fragility:** Soft (2–2.5) — scratches easily; damaged by heat and solvents.

**Synergies:**
- Citrine (`citrine`): Both carry golden, solar warmth. Citrine draws abundance; amber brings cheer and vitality.
- Carnelian (`carnelian`): Two warm orange stones of the Sacral. Carnelian drives action; amber brings steady warmth and confidence.
- Petrified Wood (`petrified-wood`): Both are gifts of ancient trees. Petrified wood grounds in deep time; amber holds its light.

**Conflicts:**
- Black Tourmaline (`black-tourmaline`): Amber radiates warm, outward energy; black tourmaline pulls energy down and seals it. Together the effect can feel muted.

**Grid role:** Warmth and vitality stone in Sacral and Solar Plexus grids

## Orange Calcite

**Id:** `orange-calcite`

**Alt names:** Calcite, Honey Calcite, Amber Calcite

**Mineral family:** Calcite (carbonate)

**Chemical formula:** CaCO₃

**Crystal system:** Trigonal (hexagonal)

**Mohs hardness:** 3

**Luster:** Vitreous to dull

**Transparency:** Transparent, translucent or opaque

**Color range:** Pale to deep orange and honey. Pure calcite is colourless or white; impurities give the orange colour.

**Origins:** Mexico, United States (Tennessee), Worldwide (limestone, marble and caves)

**Rarity:** Common

**Ethical sourcing:** Calcite is one of the most common rock-forming minerals, found in limestone, marble and caves. No specific labour or environmental issues were found for orange calcite. "Orange calcite" is a colour name, not a separate mineral.

**Simulant warnings:** Low fake risk. To confirm calcite: a steel knife scratches it (hardness 3); a drop of dilute acid makes it fizz as carbon dioxide is released; and it breaks along three perfect cleavage planes into rhombs. The acid test damages the surface, so test only a hidden spot. No source was found on dyed or waxed orange calcite.

**Short intention:** Warm the belly fire and play again

**Intention:** Creativity & Joy

**Chakras:** Sacral

**Element:** Fire

**Planet:** Sun

**Day of week:** Sunday

**Zodiac:** Leo, Cancer

**Numerology:** 2

**Polarity:** Yang

**Solfeggio frequency:** 417 Hz

**Energy type:** Activating

**Emotional:**
- Lifts low mood and brings playfulness
- Helps release shame and emotional blocks
- Encourages joy and enthusiasm
- Supports emotional recovery after a hard time

**Mental:**
- Stimulates creative ideas
- Helps overcome procrastination
- Encourages new approaches to old problems
- Supports learning through play

**Spiritual:**
- A main Sacral chakra stone in many crystal teaching lists
- Encourages joyful, embodied spirituality
- Supports creative flow and pleasure
- Clears stagnant energy, as calcite does in crystal tradition

**Physical:**
- Associated in crystal practice with vitality and energy
- Traditionally linked to the lower belly and reproductive area (Sacral chakra)
- Associated with digestion in crystal practice
- These are metaphysical beliefs, not medical claims

**Vedic tradition:** No classical Jyotish basis.

**Tcm use:** No traditional Chinese materia medica use found for orange calcite.

**Five element theory:** Corresponds to Fire (orange warmth) with Earth (its origin in limestone).

**Feng shui bagua:** Children & Creativity (Dui), Fame & Reputation (Li)

**Feng shui placement:** West area for creativity and children. Play rooms, studios and kitchens. Keep away from damp places and acids.

**Ancient lore:** Calcite defines hardness 3 on the Mohs scale. Clear calcite ("Iceland spar") shows strong double refraction, and calcite in its many colours has long been used in carving and building stone, since limestone and marble are made of it.

**Indigenous traditions:** No documented traditions specific to orange calcite were found.

**Meditation guidance:** Lie down and place orange calcite just below the navel. Picture a warm orange glow, like late-afternoon sun, filling the belly. With each breath, let the glow loosen tension and invite play. Ask: "What would I create if I were not afraid?" Write down the first answer.

**Body placement:** Below the navel (Sacral) for creativity and joy; held in the hand during creative work.

**Space placement:** Studio, play room or kitchen. West side of the home. Keep dry.

**Elixir safety:** Use with Caution

**Elixir notes:** Calcite dissolves in acids, even weak acids such as vinegar or citrus. Never place it in acidic drinks. Use the indirect method only.

**Jewelry recommendations:** Too soft (hardness 3) for rings and bracelets. Best as a tumbled pocket stone, a carved piece or a pendant worn with care.

**Safe cleansing:**
- Dry brush or soft cloth
- Moonlight
- Sound bath
- Smudging
- Visualization

**Unsafe cleansing:**
- Vinegar, lemon or any acid
- Long soaking in water
- Salt (scratches the surface)
- Rough handling (perfect cleavage)

**Charging methods:**
- Short morning sunlight
- Sound frequency 417 Hz
- Creative work done with it nearby
- Carnelian nearby

**Storage guidance:** Store separately in a soft pouch, away from harder stones and damp places. Handle gently; it splits along cleavage planes when knocked.

**Cautions:** Soft and splits easily along cleavage planes. Dissolves in acids.

**Toxicity:** Non-toxic (calcium carbonate). Safe to handle.

**Fragility:** Soft (hardness 3) with perfect cleavage in three directions.

**Synergies:**
- Carnelian (`carnelian`): Two Sacral stones. Carnelian drives action and courage; orange calcite brings joy and playfulness.
- Amber (`amber`): Both bring warm, golden-orange energy. Amber holds steady warmth; orange calcite lifts the mood.
- Sunstone (`sunstone`): Both carry sunny, uplifting energy. Sunstone builds confidence; orange calcite frees creative play.

**Conflicts:**
- Lepidolite (`lepidolite`): Lepidolite slows and sedates; orange calcite energizes and stimulates. Together the effect can feel muddled.

**Grid role:** Sacral centre stone in creativity and joy grids

## Pearl

**Id:** `pearl`

**Alt names:** Mutiara, South Sea Pearl, Cultured Pearl, Tahitian Pearl, Margarita, Zhen Zhu (珍珠), Moti, Mukta

**Mineral family:** Organic — nacre made by molluscs

**Chemical formula:** CaCO₃ (nacre) + organic matter

**Crystal system:** None (organic)

**Mohs hardness:** 2.5–4.5

**Luster:** Pearly (nacreous)

**Transparency:** Opaque

**Color range:** White, cream, silver, champagne, gold (South Sea); grey to black (Tahitian)

**Origins:** Indonesia (South Sea pearls — including Raja Ampat, Lombok, Sulawesi, Maluku, Papua), Australia, Philippines, Myanmar, French Polynesia (Tahitian pearls)

**Rarity:** Common

**Ethical sourcing:** Indonesia is the largest producer of South Sea pearls, with about 43% of world supply (government data cited by Mongabay). All Indonesian commercial pearl production is hatchery-based (FAO), so farms do not depend on wild oysters. The South Sea pearl oyster (Pinctada maxima) is not a protected species in Indonesia. Cultured pearls are common; natural pearls (formed with no human help) are very rare. Pearl is animal-derived — note this for vegan users.

**Simulant warnings:** Imitations include glass, plastic and shell beads with a pearly coating. The Majorica imitation (a glass bead with an iridescent coating) is the most widely sold. The tooth test (cultured pearls feel gritty, imitations feel smooth) is only an indication: GIA warns it can damage pearls, and some imitations are made gritty. Look at the drill hole with a loupe: a chipped coating shows an imitation. Labs use X-radiography to separate natural from cultured pearls and to detect imitations, filled pearls and dyed pearls.

**Short intention:** Turn irritation into beauty, layer by layer

**Intention:** Purity & Emotional Calm

**Chakras:** Heart, Crown

**Element:** Water

**Planet:** Moon

**Day of week:** Monday

**Zodiac:** Cancer, Gemini

**Numerology:** 2

**Polarity:** Yin

**Solfeggio frequency:** 639 Hz

**Energy type:** Calming

**Emotional:**
- Calms strong emotions and brings gentle peace
- Supports patience and emotional maturity
- Symbolizes turning pain into something beautiful
- Encourages self-acceptance and grace

**Mental:**
- Supports calm, clear thinking
- Encourages integrity and sincerity
- Helps focus attention inward
- Supports patient work done layer by layer

**Spiritual:**
- In Vedic tradition, the navaratna gem of the Moon (Chandra)
- A symbol of purity and wisdom in many cultures
- Linked to the sea and the lunar tides
- Encourages a soft, receptive, devotional heart

**Physical:**
- In traditional Chinese medicine, Zhen Zhu is used to "sedate the Heart" and calm palpitations
- Used in traditional Chinese practice as a powder on the skin and eyes
- Folk tradition links pearl with calm sleep
- These are traditional beliefs, not medical claims

**Vedic tradition:** Pearl (Moti, Sanskrit mukta) is the navaratna gem of the Moon (Chandra). Moonstone is the usual upratna (substitute). (Sources are modern gem retailers; no classical text citation was found.)

**Tcm use:** Named in the Chinese materia medica as Zhen Zhu 珍珠 (Margarita). Described as sweet, salty and cold, entering the Heart and Liver. Used to "sedate the Heart", treat palpitations and childhood convulsions, on the eyes as a powder, and to "generate flesh" on ulcers. This is a traditional belief system, not clinical medicine.

**Five element theory:** Corresponds to Water (the sea) and Metal (its white lustre).

**Feng shui bagua:** Love & Relationships (Kun), Children & Creativity (Dui)

**Feng shui placement:** Southwest area for love and partnership. Bedroom for calm. Keep away from kitchens and bathrooms (steam, acids and cosmetics).

**Ancient lore:** Pearl is the June birthstone on the modern American list of 1912; alexandrite was added in 1952, and moonstone is also listed. Pearls have been prized as jewels for thousands of years across Asia, the Middle East and Europe.

**Indigenous traditions:** Indonesia's pearl farming regions, including Lombok and Raja Ampat, supply South Sea pearls worldwide. No documented Indonesian folk beliefs about pearls (mutiara) were found.

**Meditation guidance:** Hold a pearl in your palm. Remember that it began as an irritation the oyster covered, layer by layer, with nacre. Bring to mind one thing that troubles you. With each breath, picture a soft, pearly layer of patience covering it. Rest in the calm that forms.

**Body placement:** Worn at the throat or over the heart, the traditional way. Crown for calm meditation.

**Space placement:** Bedroom or a quiet meditation corner. Away from steam, perfume and cleaning products.

**Elixir safety:** Avoid

**Elixir notes:** Do not use for elixirs. Nacre is calcium carbonate and dissolves in acids, and liquids can damage lustre.

**Jewelry recommendations:** The classic necklace and earring gem. Soft (about 2.5–4.5), so it scratches easily. Put pearls on last, after perfume, hairspray and makeup; wipe after wearing; store flat and separately.

**Safe cleansing:**
- Soft dry cloth
- Moonlight
- Sound bath
- Visualization

**Unsafe cleansing:**
- Vinegar, lemon or any acid
- Salt
- Perfume and cosmetics
- Abrasives
- Ultrasonic or steam cleaners
- Heat

**Charging methods:**
- Moonlight, especially the full moon
- Wearing against the skin
- Sound frequency 639 Hz
- Moonstone nearby

**Storage guidance:** Store flat in a soft pouch, away from harder stones. Do not hang pearl strands for long periods. Do not seal in plastic for years; pearls need a little moisture.

**Cautions:** Soft and damaged by acids, perfume and cosmetics. "Pearls" and shells from protected species are illegal to trade in Indonesia: Hippopus giant clams (kima), Cassis cornuta (kepala kambing), Charonia tritonis (triton) and Nautilus (Permen LHK P.106/2018). Giant clams and nautilus are CITES Appendix II. Pinctada maxima pearls are legal.

**Toxicity:** Non-toxic. Safe to handle and wear.

**Fragility:** Soft (2.5–4.5) — scratches easily; damaged by acids and heat.

**Synergies:**
- Moonstone (`moonstone`): Moonstone is the Vedic substitute for pearl, and both belong to the Moon. Together they deepen calm and intuition.
- Larimar (`larimar`): Both carry the gentle energy of the sea. Larimar soothes; pearl brings grace and patience.
- Rose Quartz (`rose-quartz`): Both are soft, loving Heart stones. Rose quartz opens the heart; pearl calms and purifies it.

**Conflicts:**
- Pyrite (`pyrite`): Pyrite is hard, fiery and driving; pearl is soft, lunar and calming. Pyrite can also scratch pearls when stored together.

**Grid role:** Lunar centre stone in calm and love grids

## Petrified Wood

**Id:** `petrified-wood`

**Alt names:** Batu Sempur, Fosil Kayu, Kayu Fosil, Silicified Wood, Kayu Terkersikkan

**Mineral family:** Fossil — wood replaced by silica (chalcedony, opal or quartz)

**Chemical formula:** Mostly SiO₂ with impurities (iron gives colour; carbon gives black)

**Crystal system:** Varies (quartz is trigonal; opal is amorphous)

**Mohs hardness:** 7

**Luster:** Dull to vitreous when polished

**Transparency:** Opaque

**Color range:** Pale white, jet black, brown, yellow, red and greenish; polished pieces often show tree rings and bark texture

**Origins:** Indonesia (Lebak, Banten — Gajrug, Maja, Sajira, Gunung Kencana), Indonesia (Merangin, Jambi — protected geopark), United States, Madagascar

**Rarity:** Common

**Ethical sourcing:** Banten petrified wood ("batu sempur") is abundant, but the Banten energy and mineral agency warns that supply may run out after decades of digging, and calls for it to be conserved. "Possibly thousands of containers" of raw Banten fossil wood were exported before 2005. Do not buy pieces taken from geosites such as Merangin Geopark in Jambi, where Early Permian fossil trees (about 290–300 million years old) are protected. In the USA, personal collection on BLM land is limited to 25 lb plus one piece per day and 250 lb per year, and collected wood may not be sold.

**Simulant warnings:** Low fake risk. Banten craftsmen grind and polish pieces until they are "glossy and smooth like marble" — this is finishing, not fakery. No source was found on dyed or resin-stabilised petrified wood. Ask where a piece was collected, because legal origin matters more than authenticity for this stone.

**Short intention:** Stand rooted in deep time

**Intention:** Grounding & Patience

**Chakras:** Root

**Element:** Earth

**Planet:** Saturn

**Day of week:** Saturday

**Zodiac:** Capricorn, Taurus

**Numerology:** 4

**Polarity:** Yang

**Solfeggio frequency:** 396 Hz

**Energy type:** Grounding

**Emotional:**
- Brings deep calm and a sense of stability
- Helps put present worries in a long perspective
- Supports patience during slow change
- Encourages resilience after loss

**Mental:**
- Supports steady, practical thinking
- Encourages long-term planning
- Helps finish slow, careful work
- Supports learning from the past

**Spiritual:**
- Links the wearer to ancient forests and deep time
- Associated in crystal practice with ancestral connection
- A stone of transformation: living wood turned to stone
- Grounds spiritual work into the body and the earth

**Physical:**
- Associated in crystal practice with strength and endurance
- Traditionally linked to the bones and back
- A Banten seller says fossil wood furniture "brings coolness" to a room (a modern claim)
- These are metaphysical beliefs, not medical claims

**Vedic tradition:** No classical Jyotish basis.

**Tcm use:** No traditional Chinese materia medica use found.

**Five element theory:** Corresponds to Wood turned to Earth — growth transformed into stability.

**Feng shui bagua:** Family & Health (Zhen), Knowledge & Wisdom (Gen)

**Feng shui placement:** East area for family and ancestry. Large slabs and furniture suit living rooms and entrances. Garden pieces work well near trees.

**Ancient lore:** Petrified wood forms when buried wood is slowly replaced by silica, keeping the shape of rings and bark. At Merangin, Jambi, fossil trees including Araucarioxylon trunks from the Early Permian Mengkarang Formation are about 290–300 million years old. In Banten, pieces range from a few centimetres to whole trunks up to 1.5 m across and tens of metres long.

**Indigenous traditions:** In Banten, petrified wood is called "batu sempur", after the sempur tree, which people say is the most common fossil wood. Sajira, Lebak is a craft centre for polished fossil-wood furniture and ornaments, and Baduy people help supply raw material to one craft seller. No documented folklore was found.

**Meditation guidance:** Sit with your back against a wall or tree and hold petrified wood in your lap. Picture the living tree it once was, then the millions of years of slow change. Let your breath slow to match that pace. Feel roots grow from your spine into the earth. Stay until the mind is still.

**Body placement:** Root for grounding; held in both hands during stressful times; carried in a pocket.

**Space placement:** Living room, entrance or garden. Large slabs as tables or bookends. East side of the home for family and ancestry.

**Elixir safety:** Generally Safe

**Elixir notes:** Polished silica pieces are generally safe for direct elixirs. For iron-stained or rough pieces, use the indirect method.

**Jewelry recommendations:** Durable silica (about 7) — suitable for cabochons, pendants and beads. Heavier pieces suit display rather than wear.

**Safe cleansing:**
- Water and soft brush
- Earth burial
- Moonlight
- Sunlight
- Smudging
- Sound bath

**Unsafe cleansing:**
- Dry cutting or grinding (silica dust)
- Dropping large logs (they can split along old fractures)

**Charging methods:**
- Earth contact
- Placing under a living tree
- Sound frequency 396 Hz
- Smoky quartz nearby

**Storage guidance:** Durable. Support large logs and slabs well; they are heavy and can split along old fractures.

**Cautions:** Do not collect or buy wood taken from protected geosites such as Merangin Geopark. Silica dust from cutting is a lung hazard. Large pieces are heavy.

**Toxicity:** Non-toxic when polished. Silica dust from cutting or grinding is a respirable hazard.

**Fragility:** Low — hard and durable, but large pieces can split along old fractures.

**Synergies:**
- Amber (`amber`): Both are gifts of ancient trees. Petrified wood grounds in deep time; amber holds the forest's light.
- Smoky Quartz (`smoky-quartz`): Both ground and stabilize. Smoky quartz clears heavy energy; petrified wood brings patience and endurance.
- Klawing Jasper (`klawing-jasper`): Two Indonesian earth stones. Klawing jasper protects; petrified wood roots you in time and ancestry.

**Conflicts:**
- Moldavite (`moldavite`): Moldavite brings sudden, intense change; petrified wood brings slow, steady patience. Together they pull in opposite directions.

**Grid role:** Foundation and anchor stone in grounding and ancestor grids

## Bumblebee Calcite

**Id:** `bumblebee-calcite`

**Alt names:** Bumblebee Jasper, Eclipse Stone, Eclipse Jasper, Fumarole Jasper, Mustard Jasper, Batu Badar Belerang

**Mineral family:** Calcite rock with realgar (contains arsenic)

**Chemical formula:** CaCO₃ (calcite) + As₄S₄ (realgar) + FeS₂ (pyrite)

**Crystal system:** Rock (calcite is trigonal)

**Mohs hardness:** 3

**Luster:** Vitreous to dull when polished

**Transparency:** Opaque

**Color range:** Bands of yellow, orange, grey, white and black

**Origins:** Indonesia (West Java — listed under Papandayan volcano, Garut, and Kuningan Regency; exact source unresolved)

**Rarity:** Uncommon

**Ethical sourcing:** TOXIC STONE — CONTAINS ARSENIC. "Bumblebee Jasper" is a trade name. Lab tests (XRD and wet chemistry, reported by mindat manager Alfredo Petrov) show it is not jasper but limestone made mainly of calcite, coloured by realgar, an arsenic sulfide. An ICP-MS test found it "quite rich in arsenic". Cutting and polishing creates arsenic-bearing dust, which is a health risk for cutters in Indonesia and Jaipur. Small-scale local digging is reported; no labour or environmental study was found.

**Simulant warnings:** Chinese orpiment (another arsenic sulfide) and other yellow-banded stones are sold under the same name. A lab separates them with XRD or Raman, and measures arsenic with ICP-MS. Do NOT do acid tests at home: acid plus arsenic sulfide is hazardous. No source was found on dyed or resin-stabilised pieces.

**Short intention:** Admire the colour — keep it out of mouths and water

**Intention:** Confidence & Creativity (display only)

**Chakras:** Sacral, Solar Plexus

**Element:** Fire

**Planet:** Sun

**Day of week:** Sunday

**Zodiac:** Taurus, Leo

**Numerology:** 3

**Polarity:** Yang

**Solfeggio frequency:** 528 Hz

**Energy type:** Activating

**Emotional:**
- Retailers describe it as a stone of confidence and enthusiasm
- Associated in modern crystal practice with lifting low mood
- Bright yellow colour linked to optimism
- Best enjoyed as a display stone, not a handled worry stone

**Mental:**
- Associated with focus and manifestation in retailer texts
- Linked to creativity and new ideas
- Encourages decisive action
- Supports motivation (modern attribution)

**Spiritual:**
- Modern retailers link it to the Sacral and Solar Plexus chakras
- Associated with volcanic Fire energy
- Formed near volcanic activity in West Java
- Use from a distance: place on a shelf, not on the body

**Physical:**
- NO health use. It contains arsenic and must never be ingested or used in water
- Do not let children handle or mouth it
- Wash hands after handling
- Any healing claims are modern retail claims with no evidence

**Vedic tradition:** No classical basis.

**Tcm use:** None for this stone. (Realgar itself, "xionghuang", is a TCM substance, but it is toxic and this stone must not be used medicinally.)

**Five element theory:** Corresponds to Fire (volcanic origin, yellow-orange colour) and Earth (limestone).

**Feng shui bagua:** Fame & Reputation (Li) — display only

**Feng shui placement:** Only on a high shelf or in a closed display case, out of reach of children and pets. Away from direct light.

**Ancient lore:** No ancient lore. The stone entered the commercial market in the 1990s. The name "Eclipse Stone" is said to have started in Jaipur, India, where much of it is cut.

**Indigenous traditions:** No documented Sundanese or Indonesian folklore was found. The local market term "batu badar belerang" (sulfur badar stone) is reported only by a retailer.

**Meditation guidance:** Do not hold or place this stone on the body for meditation. Instead, set it on a shelf in front of you and gaze at its bands of colour. Picture the warm yellow light filling the solar plexus. For hands-on Solar Plexus work, use citrine or orange calcite instead.

**Body placement:** Not recommended. Do not wear it against the skin for long periods, and never put it in the mouth.

**Space placement:** Closed display case or high shelf, away from children, pets, food areas and direct sunlight.

**Elixir safety:** Avoid

**Elixir notes:** NEVER use for elixirs, direct or indirect. It contains arsenic sulfide (realgar), and calcite is attacked by acids.

**Jewelry recommendations:** Not recommended for jewellery. It is soft (about 3), contains arsenic, and wear can release particles. If you own beads, do not let children handle them.

**Safe cleansing:**
- Dry soft cloth
- Sound bath
- Smudging at a distance
- Moonlight
- Visualization

**Unsafe cleansing:**
- Water soaking (may release fine particles)
- Salt (abrasive)
- Sun charging (light breaks down realgar)
- Acids such as vinegar or lemon
- Heat (realgar releases arsenic fumes)
- Ultrasonic cleaning, tumbling or polishing at home

**Charging methods:**
- Sound frequency 528 Hz
- Visualization
- Placing near citrine (not touching food or water)

**Storage guidance:** Store in a closed container out of direct light, away from children and pets. Light slowly breaks down realgar into yellow pararealgar (Douglass et al. 1992, American Mineralogist).

**Cautions:** CONTAINS ARSENIC. Never ingest, never use in water or elixirs, never cut, sand or grind it. Keep away from children and pets. Wash hands after handling. Polished, intact stones are low risk to touch.

**Toxicity:** Toxic — contains realgar (arsenic sulfide). Harmful if swallowed or if dust is inhaled. Intact polished stone is low risk to touch; wash hands after.

**Fragility:** Soft (about 3); scratches easily; fibrous calcite can split.

**Synergies:**
- Citrine (`citrine`): Use citrine as the hands-on, non-toxic stone for the same sunny Solar Plexus intention, and keep bumblebee calcite on display nearby.
- Orange Calcite (`orange-calcite`): Orange calcite is the safe calcite for Sacral work; pair it with bumblebee calcite on a display shelf for colour and theme.
- Black Tourmaline (`black-tourmaline`): A grounding display partner that balances bumblebee calcite's bright, fiery look.

**Conflicts:** none

**Grid role:** Display-only accent; do not use in grids that involve water or handling

## Diamond

**Id:** `diamond`

**Alt names:** Intan, Berlian, Galuh, Intan Martapura, Galuh Banjar, Adamas, Vajra, Heera

**Mineral family:** Native element (carbon)

**Chemical formula:** C

**Crystal system:** Cubic (isometric)

**Mohs hardness:** 10

**Luster:** Adamantine

**Transparency:** Transparent to opaque

**Color range:** Colourless, yellow and brown; rarely blue, pink, green or black. Cempaka guides describe "clear, milk and black" stones.

**Origins:** Indonesia (Cempaka, Banjarbaru, South Kalimantan — cut and sold in Martapura), Botswana, Russia, Canada, Australia, South Africa

**Rarity:** Uncommon

**Ethical sourcing:** Buy diamonds with a Kimberley Process certificate for the rough, and ask for origin information. Indonesia joined the Kimberley Process in 2005 but reports zero official production, so Cempaka diamonds do not appear in KP statistics. About 1,000 people mine diamonds by hand in South Kalimantan, many in poverty. A UGM study found heavy environmental damage at one Sungai Tiung mine site. Cempaka gravels also yield gold; no source confirms mercury use in diamond panning there.

**Simulant warnings:** Lab-grown diamond (CVD/HPHT) is real diamond and passes a thermal "diamond tester"; only a gem lab can identify it. Moissanite also passes basic thermal testers; a thermal + electrical tester or strong doubling under a loupe separates it. Cubic zirconia and glass fail thermal testers, and CZ is much heavier. Some coloured diamonds are irradiated. Home tests are screening only — get a lab report for valuable stones.

**Short intention:** Clear light, unbreakable will

**Intention:** Clarity & Strength

**Chakras:** Crown

**Element:** Fire

**Planet:** Venus

**Day of week:** Friday

**Zodiac:** Aries, Leo, Libra

**Numerology:** 1

**Polarity:** Yang

**Solfeggio frequency:** 963 Hz

**Energy type:** Amplifying

**Emotional:**
- Symbolizes lasting love and commitment
- Encourages courage and resilience
- Supports clarity in relationships
- Brings a sense of inner brilliance and worth

**Mental:**
- Associated with mental clarity and focus
- Supports clear, firm decisions
- Encourages integrity and honesty
- Helps cut through confusion

**Spiritual:**
- In Vedic tradition, one of the navaratna, linked to Venus (Shukra)
- Amplifies intention, as clear crystals do in crystal practice
- Symbol of purity and the light of the Crown
- In Banjar tradition, a stone treated with deep respect and taboos at the mine

**Physical:**
- Associated in crystal practice with strength and vitality
- Traditionally linked to endurance
- No medical evidence supports any health use
- These are traditional beliefs, not medical claims

**Vedic tradition:** Diamond (vajra, heera) is one of the navaratna, the nine gems of Jyotish, linked to Venus (Shukra).

**Tcm use:** No traditional Chinese materia medica use found.

**Five element theory:** Corresponds to Metal (hardness, brilliance and clarity).

**Feng shui bagua:** Love & Relationships (Kun), Fame & Reputation (Li)

**Feng shui placement:** Worn as jewellery rather than placed. Small rough diamonds can sit on a Crown or meditation altar.

**Ancient lore:** Diamond is the April birthstone on the modern list. In South Kalimantan, the largest Cempaka find is the "Intan Trisakti" (166.75 carats, 26 August 1965); a USGS deposit record notes that the largest diamond found in these paleochannel gravels weighed 167 carats.

**Indigenous traditions:** In Banjar tradition at the Cempaka mines, saying "intan" or "berlian" at the panning site is taboo; it is believed to make the diamond go away, so miners say "galuh" instead (Banjarbaru city government). Other taboos reported by RRI include no harsh words, sneezing, hands on hips, whistling or relieving oneself at the site, and choosing a good day to start. Blog sources add substitute words (a snake is called "akar", root) and say entering the pit is called "naik" (go up). Named Cempaka stones include Intan Trisakti, Galuh Cempaka, Puteri Malu and Galuh Pumpung.

**Meditation guidance:** Hold a diamond (or wear a diamond ring) and look into its light. Picture a clear white beam entering the crown of the head and moving down the spine. With each breath, let the light cut through confusion. Set one clear, firm intention and repeat it three times.

**Body placement:** Worn as a ring, pendant or earrings. Crown for meditation.

**Space placement:** Mostly worn, not placed. A rough diamond can sit on a meditation altar.

**Elixir safety:** Safe

**Elixir notes:** Diamond is inert and insoluble. Remove metal settings before making an elixir.

**Jewelry recommendations:** Ideal for daily rings and all jewellery (hardness 10). It has perfect cleavage, so a sharp knock on an edge can still chip or split it.

**Safe cleansing:**
- Warm soapy water
- Running water
- Salt
- Sunlight
- Smudging
- Sound bath

**Unsafe cleansing:**
- Harsh cleaning of fracture-filled stones
- Torch heat (burns the surface)

**Charging methods:**
- Sunlight
- Full moon
- Clear quartz cluster
- Sound frequency 963 Hz

**Storage guidance:** Store separately — diamond scratches all other gems and other diamonds.

**Cautions:** Hard but can chip along cleavage. Lab-grown diamond and moissanite pass basic testers; get a lab report for valuable stones. Ask for Kimberley Process paperwork.

**Toxicity:** Non-toxic (inert carbon).

**Fragility:** Hardest mineral, but perfect octahedral cleavage means a sharp knock can split it.

**Synergies:**
- Clear Quartz (`clear-quartz`): Both are clear amplifiers. Clear quartz is the accessible companion; diamond adds lasting brilliance and focus.
- Ruby (`ruby`): Two navaratna gems. Ruby brings passion and vitality; diamond brings clarity and commitment.
- Emerald (`emerald`): Two classic precious gems of love. Emerald brings heart-centred loyalty; diamond adds strength and permanence.

**Conflicts:** none

**Grid role:** Centre amplifier in clarity and commitment grids

## Topaz

**Id:** `topaz`

**Alt names:** Imperial Topaz, Precious Topaz, Sherry Topaz, Blue Topaz, Topas

**Mineral family:** Nesosilicate

**Chemical formula:** Al₂SiO₄(F,OH)₂

**Crystal system:** Orthorhombic

**Mohs hardness:** 8

**Luster:** Vitreous

**Transparency:** Transparent to translucent

**Color range:** Colourless, yellow, sherry, orange, pink, red and light blue. Strong blue is almost always irradiated. Imperial topaz is orange-yellow to reddish-orange.

**Origins:** Brazil (Ouro Preto, Minas Gerais — imperial topaz), Indonesia (non-commercial, in Cempaka diamond gravels), Worldwide

**Rarity:** Common

**Ethical sourcing:** Most blue topaz is colourless topaz irradiated in reactors or accelerators, then held back until the radioactivity falls (one patent says 4–12 months). In the USA, the first sale of neutron-irradiated gems needs an NRC licence; the NRC says wearing legally sold irradiated gems is not harmful (dose at the highest allowed level is about 0.03 millirem per year). The NRC knows of some stones sold without the required licence between 2002 and 2007. No labour data was found for Ouro Preto. Natural imperial topaz is rare; blue and colourless topaz are common.

**Simulant warnings:** Assume blue topaz is treated unless a lab says otherwise. "Mystic" topaz is colourless topaz with a thin metal-oxide coating that GIA says is "not very durable"; look for worn facet edges and scratches. Pink topaz is often heated imperial topaz (lab only). Citrine and smoky quartz have been sold as "Madeira topaz" or "smoky topaz"; topaz is much heavier (SG about 3.5 vs 2.65 for quartz).

**Short intention:** Shine with warm, generous confidence

**Intention:** Joy & Abundance

**Chakras:** Solar Plexus, Throat

**Element:** Fire

**Planet:** Jupiter

**Day of week:** Thursday

**Zodiac:** Sagittarius, Scorpio

**Solfeggio frequency:** 528 Hz

- Encourages generous, open communication (blue)
- Helps focus energy where it is needed

- Golden topaz links to the Solar Plexus; blue topaz to the Throat

- Associated in crystal practice with manifestation

- Associated in crystal practice with vitality
- Traditionally linked to digestion (golden topaz)
- No medical evidence supports any health use
- These are metaphysical beliefs, not medical claims

**Vedic tradition:** In modern Indian gem practice, yellow topaz (sunela) is used as an upratna (substitute) for yellow sapphire (pukhraj), the gem of Jupiter. No classical text source was found.

**Tcm use:** No traditional Chinese materia medica use found.

**Five element theory:** Golden topaz corresponds to Fire and Earth; blue topaz to Water.

**Feng shui bagua:** Wealth & Prosperity (Xun), Fame & Reputation (Li)

**Feng shui placement:** Southeast for prosperity. Keep golden and imperial topaz out of direct sun to prevent fading.

**Ancient lore:** Topaz is the November birthstone, and blue topaz is a modern December stone. Imperial topaz has been mined near Ouro Preto, Brazil, since the early 1800s (Capão mine). Heating Ouro Preto topaz above about 450 °C fades its orange-red colour, which is how much pink topaz is made.

**Indigenous traditions:** No documented folk traditions were found. In Indonesia, topaz appears as a waste mineral in the Cempaka diamond gravels and in batu akik lists as "batu topas".

**Meditation guidance:** Hold golden topaz at the solar plexus and picture warm sunlight spreading through the body. Breathe in confidence; breathe out doubt. For calm speech, use blue topaz at the throat instead and picture clear blue water.

**Body placement:** Solar plexus (golden); throat (blue); worn as rings, pendants and earrings.

**Space placement:** Desk or office for success. Out of direct sunlight.

**Elixir safety:** Generally Safe

**Elixir notes:** Natural, uncoated topaz is generally safe; the indirect method is preferred. Use the indirect method only for coated ("mystic") topaz, because water and abrasion damage the coating.

**Jewelry recommendations:** Good for rings and all jewellery (hardness 8), but it has perfect cleavage and "poor" toughness (GIA), so protect it from hard knocks. Avoid steam and ultrasonic cleaners.

**Safe cleansing:**
- Warm soapy water
- Moonlight
- Sound bath
- Smudging

**Unsafe cleansing:**
- Steam or ultrasonic cleaners
- Sun charging of yellow, brown or imperial topaz (fades)

**Charging methods:**

- Sound frequency 528 Hz

**Storage guidance:** Store in a soft pouch away from harder stones and out of light. Protect from knocks because of cleavage.

**Cautions:** Perfect cleavage — avoid knocks. Yellow-brown topaz can fade in sun or heat. Most blue topaz is irradiated; buy from sellers who follow licensing rules.

**Toxicity:** Non-toxic. Contains fluorine bound in the crystal; no practical hazard.

**Fragility:** Hard (8) but perfect basal cleavage and poor toughness.

**Synergies:**

- Aquamarine (`aquamarine`): Blue topaz and aquamarine both calm the Throat and support clear, gentle speech.

**Conflicts:** none

**Grid role:** Abundance and success stone in prosperity grids

## Bloodstone

**Id:** `bloodstone`

**Alt names:** Heliotrope, Stone of Babylon, Plasma (no red spots)

**Mineral family:** Chalcedony / jasper (microcrystalline quartz)

**Chemical formula:** SiO₂

**Crystal system:** Trigonal (microcrystalline)

**Mohs hardness:** 6.5

**Luster:** Vitreous to waxy

**Transparency:** Translucent to opaque

**Color range:** Dark green with red (sometimes yellow) spots. Pliny: "leek-green in colour, but … marked with blood-red streaks".

**Origins:** India (Deccan Traps), Historically Ethiopia, Africa and Cyprus (Pliny)

**Rarity:** Common

**Ethical sourcing:** No sourcing issues were found. Modern supply is mostly from India. "African bloodstone" (setonite) and "Dragon's Blood" (jasper with epidote) are different rocks sold under similar names.

**Simulant warnings:** No source was found on dyed bloodstone. A gem lab identifies it by refractive index (about 1.53–1.54) and specific gravity (about 2.6). Red-spotted green Klawing jasper from Purbalingga, Java, is sometimes sold as bloodstone.

**Chakras:** Root, Heart

**Element:** Earth

**Planet:** Mars

**Day of week:** Tuesday

**Zodiac:** Aries, Pisces

**Tcm use:** No traditional Chinese materia medica use found.

**Five element theory:** Corresponds to Wood (green) with Fire (red spots).

**Feng shui bagua:** Family & Health (Zhen), Fame & Reputation (Li)

**Feng shui placement:** East area for family and health. Carried in a pocket for courage.

**Ancient lore:** Pliny the Elder (Natural History 37.165) describes heliotrope as "leek-green … marked with blood-red streaks". Dropped in water in sunlight, it was said to turn the light blood-red; out of water it showed solar eclipses. Pliny called the Magi's claim that the stone, the heliotrope plant and prayers make the wearer invisible "quite the most blatant instance of effrontery". Albertus Magnus called it the "stone of Babylon", and it appears as an invisibility stone in Boccaccio's Decameron. A Christian legend says it formed when Christ's blood fell on green jasper, but no primary source for this was found. Bloodstone is the traditional March birthstone.

**Indigenous traditions:** No documented regional traditions were found.

**Meditation guidance:** Hold bloodstone in your dominant hand. Picture deep green roots growing from your feet into the earth, with red sparks of courage rising up into the heart. Breathe steadily. Name the challenge you face and say: "I meet this with courage and kindness."

**Body placement:** Root for grounding; heart for courage; carried in a pocket.

**Space placement:** Entrance or east area of the home. Desk during stressful work.

**Elixir safety:** Generally Safe

**Elixir notes:** Silica, insoluble. Use the indirect method for dyed or resin-treated pieces.

**Jewelry recommendations:** Tough with no cleavage — good for rings, signet stones, beads and pendants.

**Safe cleansing:**
- Running water
- Smudging
- Sound bath
- Moonlight
- Earth burial

**Unsafe cleansing:**
- Long salt soaks (can dull the polish)
- Harsh heat or thermal shock

**Charging methods:**
- Sunlight (heliotrope means "sun-turner")
- Earth contact
- Sound frequency 396 Hz
- Hematite nearby

**Storage guidance:** Durable. Store with stones of similar hardness.

**Cautions:** Health claims (stopping bleeding, purifying blood) have no medical support. Several different rocks are sold as "bloodstone".

**Toxicity:** Non-toxic. Silica dust only when cutting.

**Fragility:** Low — tough, no cleavage.

**Synergies:**
- Hematite (`hematite`): Both are traditional "blood" stones of the Root. Hematite grounds; bloodstone adds courage and heart.
- Klawing Jasper (`klawing-jasper`): Red-spotted green Klawing jasper resembles bloodstone. Together they ground and protect.
- Carnelian (`carnelian`): Both bring courage. Carnelian fires up action; bloodstone keeps it grounded and kind.

**Conflicts:** none

**Grid role:** Courage and protection stone in Root and Heart grids

## Tanzanite

**Id:** `tanzanite`

**Alt names:** Blue Zoisite

**Mineral family:** Zoisite (sorosilicate, epidote group)

**Chemical formula:** Ca₂Al₃(SiO₄)(Si₂O₇)O(OH)

**Crystal system:** Orthorhombic

**Mohs hardness:** 6.5

**Luster:** Vitreous

**Transparency:** Transparent

**Color range:** Blue to violet; strongly pleochroic (shows blue, violet and reddish tones from different angles). Rough is usually brownish before heating.

**Origins:** Tanzania only (Merelani / Mirerani Hills, Simanjiro District, Manyara Region)

**Rarity:** Rare

**Ethical sourcing:** Tanzanite comes from one small area. About 40% of production was reported lost to smuggling before Tanzania built a wall (about 24 km) around the mines, opened in April 2018. Royalty compliance by small-scale miners has been very low. On 28 March 2008 heavy rain flooded the Block B pits and tunnels, killing at least 57 miners according to JCK (at least 65 according to a later occupational-injury paper). Tanzania now requires mined tanzanite to pass through the Tanzanite Exchange Centre.

**Simulant warnings:** Nearly all tanzanite is heated at low temperature to remove brown; this is standard, stable and often not disclosed. Coating is rare but exists (look for iridescence and worn facet edges). Synthetic forsterite ("Tanzanion") is the main imitation and shows strong doubling of back facets; tanzanite has RI 1.691–1.704 and SG 3.30. Glass, YAG and cubic zirconia show no pleochroism. There is no commercial lab-grown tanzanite. Home check: tilt the stone and look for the colour change between blue, violet and reddish.

- Supports study of spiritual subjects
